# Aurek: the native engine, plan and progress

This replaces the "lift PSP functions one at a time" approach of the earlier ExperimentalRewrite work. That approach left the
game PSP-shaped (MIPS register locals, MIPS stack arrays, PSP data layout, one big lock, a renderer that imitates the PSP GE) and
could not get the Vita past ~13 FPS, because the cost is spread over thousands of decompiled functions with no hotspot to fix.

**Name:** Aurek, the first letter of Aurebesh: a nod to Star Wars, to Rebellion, and to the A of their Asura engine, whose files it
reads. The logo is `engine/branding/aurek_logo.png`; it becomes the engine's identity (boot screen, icons, about page) once the
builds are acceptable. Code prefix: `ak_` / `AK_`.

**The new approach:** Aurek, a native engine in `engine/`, written per device from the start, that loads the original game files and
reproduces the game's behaviour. The decompiled game (`src/game`, `platform/common`) stays in the repository as the
**specification and test oracle only**. Nothing in `engine/` includes it, links it, or depends on its data layout.

## Rules (checked by `engine/tools/check_independence.sh`, run by every engine build)

1. `engine/` never includes or links `src/game`, `platform/common`, `platform/<device>` (the old ports) or generated decompiler output.
2. No PSP concepts in engine interfaces: no sceGu/GE state, no PSP vertex formats, no 480x272, no guest pointers or guest memory,
   no vblank pacing, no big lock, no PSP thread priorities. PSP formats exist only inside the asset loaders, which turn them into
   native structures at load (or at cook time).
3. Behaviour comes from reading the decompiled code (and Ghidra): rules, numbers, timings, AI decisions. The engine reimplements
   it with its own data structures. Where exactness matters (damage, physics, AI choices), a side-by-side check against the
   decompiled build is written, as `RS_LIFT_CHECK` did for collision.
4. Per-device code is first-class: each device has its own renderer, threading, memory and audio back end, written for that
   hardware's strengths. The shared engine is data-oriented (arrays of components, job-parallel updates) so it scales from the
   Old 3DS's 2 cores to a PC's 16+.
5. Original data is never committed. The engine reads the owner's UMD files (ISO or extracted); device-specific cooked caches are
   built from them on the device or by the PC conversion tool.
6. Owner's standing rules still apply: every performance flag, refreshed settings.txt with every build, builds overwrite the
   previous ones in `D:\SWBFRSDecomp\builds`, commit + push every change.

## Layout

```
engine/
  core/        types, log, memory arenas, file access, math, job system, settings
  assets/      loaders for the game's files: Asura containers, RSCF resources, textures, level geometry, materials,
               models, skeletons, animation, collision (PHEN), navigation (NAV1), entities (ENTI), sounds, scripts
  world/       entity storage (structure-of-arrays), spatial grid, transforms, the frame's job graph
  game/        rules (conquest, CTF, hunt, space), command posts, tickets, spawning, soldiers, weapons, vehicles, heroes, AI
  render/      device-independent scene: cameras, culling, draw lists, materials (PSP-style, lightweight PBR, full PBR)
  audio/       mixer front end, music/speech streaming
  net/         RS server protocol client, host/dedicated mode
  ui/          front end menus, HUD, text
  platform/    one folder per device: desktop (Windows/Linux/Deck), vita, 3ds, ps4, ps5
               each provides: window/present, gfx back end, input, audio out, threads/cores, files, clocks, memory
  tools/       akinspect (loads a level, prints and exports it), check_independence.sh, cooker
```

## Device targets

| Device | Frame rate | Bots | Shading | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PS Vita | 30 min, 60 goal | 64 | lightweight PBR when headroom allows | GXM, 3 cores + 4th (CapUnlocker), 960x544 |
| Old 3DS | 20 | PSP count (16 players) | PSP-style | citro3d, core 1 used, PSP controls |
| New 3DS | 30 | PSP count | PSP-style | split input (twin-stick on C-stick), cores 2/3 |
| Steam Deck | 60 | 64 | Frostbite PBR | Vulkan |
| PC (Windows/Linux) | 30/60/120/240/monitor | 128 | Frostbite PBR, optional ray tracing (RTX acceleration when present) | Vulkan |
| PS4 | 60 | 128 | Frostbite PBR | |
| PS5 | 60/120 | 128 | Frostbite PBR, optional ray tracing (no RTX checks) | |
| Server | headless | per session table | none | self-hosted matches from serversession tables |

**Frostbite PBR** = the BRDF of "Moving Frostbite to PBR" (Lagarde & de Rousiers 2014), as used in EA's Star Wars Battlefront II
(2017): renormalised Disney/Burley diffuse, GGX (Trowbridge-Reitz) NDF, height-correlated Smith GGX visibility, Schlick Fresnel,
Karis split-sum environment term with Lagarde's specular occlusion. The existing Vulkan version is in `platform/linux_test/gfx_vk.c`
(reference only; the engine's renderer gets its own).

**Parity task list:** [PARITY_TASKS.md](PARITY_TASKS.md) - every behaviour still assumed, partial or missing, and the groundwork the
lightweight PBR renderer needs. Work continues from that list.

## Stages

### Stage 1: independence (owner priority 1)

The engine plays a full match natively, on desktop first (fast iteration, Linux harness) and on Vita at each milestone.

| # | Milestone | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| 1.0 | Skeleton | `engine/` builds on desktop and Vita; independence check passes |
| 1.1 | Level assets | every level file's geometry, materials and textures load into native structures; counts match `tools/asura` |
| 1.2 | Level viewer | desktop and Vita draw a level natively (static geometry in GPU buffers, batched per material), free camera |
| 1.3 | Models, skeletons, animation | soldiers, props and vehicles load and animate |
| 1.4 | Entities, collision, navigation | ENTI objects placed, PHEN collision queries, NAV1 graph |
| 1.5 | Soldiers and weapons | one player walks, shoots, takes damage, respawns (numbers from the decompiled code) |
| 1.6 | Game rules | conquest: command posts, capture, tickets, win/lose; then CTF, hunt, space |
| 1.7 | AI | bots navigate, fight, capture; parallel per bot |
| 1.8 | Vehicles and heroes | ground, air, space vehicles; hero unlocks |
| 1.9 | Audio | effects, music, speech |
| 1.10 | Front end and HUD | menus, loadouts, colour picker, map selection, galactic conquest, campaign |
| 1.11 | Network | RS server protocol, hosting, dedicated server mode |
| 1.12 | Parity | the engine replaces the decompiled build on every device; the old ports are retired |

### Stage 2: Vita 30 FPS, 64 bots (owner priority 2)

Built in from 1.2 onward rather than tuned afterwards: frame work split into jobs across the cores, static geometry uploaded once,
few large draws, no per-draw CPU vertex conversion, textures in GXM-native formats, a per-frame budget per subsystem.

### Stage 3: Vita toward 60 FPS (owner priority 3), then lightweight PBR

### Stage 4: the other devices to their targets (3DS, Deck, PC, PS4, PS5), Frostbite PBR, ray tracing

### Stage 5: server testing, then mod support

| 2026-11-11 | 1.4 | **see-through holes fixed (owner's report).** Measured by comparing renders with and without back-face culling (pixels that change = surfaces seen from behind): models have single-sided panels meant to be seen from both sides (TIE wing plates, AT-AT plating, armour), and so does some solid level geometry (the Sullust generator, about 30,000 pixels), with no material flag marking it; re-orienting triangles by their vertex normals made it worse. So the game draws without back-face culling: culling is now off by default (AK_CULL=1 keeps it for solid level surfaces only; see-through pipelines and models are always two-sided; on the Vita the GPU removes hidden surfaces anyway). Alpha testing now follows the material flag 0x2 only: the texture-alpha guess discarded texels whose alpha means something else (the TIE wing panels went missing) |

| 2026-11-12 | 1.6 | **conquest rules, from the game's code.** Post records end with owner (0 / 1 / neutral), capture amount (10 s for one soldier on most posts, 20-30 on a few, 300 on space capital ships) and flags (bit 0: not capturable - the capital ships); every level parses. Capture (the post's update): living soldiers within 5 m, weight 1.0 each, rate capped at 5/s; the owner refills, others drain to neutral then to the capturer, a different team's partial capture is pushed back first; both teams present = 30 s contest timer, then neutral. Tickets: each death costs the dead soldier's team one; the option is 25..200 (its default lives in the profile, not found in the code - AK_TICKETS, 100); 0 tickets loses. Bleed: holding more than half the posts costs the other team a ticket every 4 s while it has more tickets than living soldiers; no posts and no living soldiers = a ticket every 0.1 s. Total loss: a team holding every post wins after 60 s ("CommandPostTotalLossTime"), 11x as fast while the other team has nobody alive. Spawn waves every 10 s ("RespawnDelay") at an owned post's team spawn. Team 1 = Republic / Alliance, team 2 = CIS / Empire (Hoth: the Alliance starts with Echo Base's four posts, the Empire one 30 s post). `game/ak_conquest` (start, update, killed, spawn); play mode: soldier health, death, respawn with the wave at the nearest owned post, posts coloured by owner, tickets / posts / capture HUD, victory / defeat. Tests: Hoth bleed 4 tickets in 20 s, capture of a Rebel post as Empire (neutral at 10 s, ours at 20 s), death + respawn on the wave; rules harness: neutral to ours 10 s, fast drain win, total-loss win at 60 s with bleed 17 tickets, contest neutral at 30 s; all 7 ground levels start with the record's owners (Hoth 11112, Kashyyyk 1-2, Mustafar 221211, Naboo 1122, Sullust 11-22, Tatooine -2112, Yavin IV 11-22). | 2026-11-12 | 1.7 | **bots.** `app/play.c`: AK_BOTS soldiers (default 15 on desktop, 63 in the Vita settings; up to 127) split between the sides, each one of its faction's own classes that the level has a body for (Hoth Empire: Biker Scout, Stormtrooper, Darktrooper; Alliance: Rebel Trooper Commando, Medium Rebel, Wookiee Warrior), carrying the faction's blaster rifle. A bot picks a post its side does not hold (nearest, with some randomness), walks there on the NAV1 graph (A*, each bot on its own line through the nodes so squads don't walk single file), stands inside the 5 m until it is theirs, then moves on; with every post held it defends one. It fights the nearest enemy it can see within the targeting range (50 m; line of sight to the chest, behind it only when within 12 m): 0.35-0.65 s to react, bursts of 0.8-2 s, aim error 3 degrees (AK_BOT_AIM), slower and strafing in a fight; stuck = jump, then skip a node. The game's own AI is not traced yet, so these numbers are the engine's. Shots carry their team (friendly fire off, the game's default) and their shooter (kills credited). Soldiers push each other apart; deaths cost tickets; the fallen come back with the 10 s spawn wave, scattered around an owned post's spawn. Far bots re-pose 8 times a second. Tests: 64 soldiers on all 7 ground levels for 90 s - no crashes, every level captures posts (Mustafar 15 captures, ended 5 - 0), Mustafar and Yavin IV finished by tickets; Hoth +0.5 ms a frame on desktop with 63 bots. Also: **laser bolts, impact flashes and command-post columns are now "star" sprites** (`ak_sprite_star`): six flat fins radiating from the axis every 60 degrees, solid along the axis and fading out at the outer edge, which is set in at both ends so the tips pinch to the axis; drawn from both sides, never turned to face the camera, they read as a solid glowing shape from any angle (36 vertices each). The format for every plane-based effect from now on. Debug: AK_DEBUG_FOLLOW_BOT=N watches bot N |

The last one out makes the vehicle team 4, neutral (089D2B60), so anyone may then board it; boarding gives it the occupant's side (089D29D8). Unused vehicles at an enemy spawn cannot be taken. Command post holograms at 75% light (`AK_POST_GLOW`); debug text one colour, black on the Vita (its blue backdrop), grey elsewhere (`AK_DEBUG_TEXT_RGB`). |

Samples (SDSM, 0882C648) carry two names the game skips. **Mixer:** SDMX (0882D090) has Master and nine groups - Weapons, Foley, "Narration " (with the space), Environmentals, Vehicles, Music, UI, Sniper_View, AI Speech - all 1.0, and three snapshots: "default" (all 1.0), "UI Dip" (0.4 but UI / Music / Narration ~1.0) and "Dipping for speech" (0.38-0.41, Music 0.6, UI 0.61, Narration 0.99); each a name, u32 0, u32 n, n x { group name, hash, volume, f32, f32, u32 }. The game moves between them with fades (088E0790): func 08A662E8 dips for speech when more than one line plays or the one playing is not AI chatter - the narrator - and goes back to "default" when it ends, 0.3 s each way (0x08AF086C); "UI Dip" (0.7 s, 0x08AF0868) is for the pause screen (none in ours yet). Narration plays in "Narration ", chatter in "AI Speech", music in "Music". **Chatter, more of the game's player (08A65690):** one AI line at a time in the whole level, none while any speech plays, and each event's own chance - its DLEV header's byte at +8 (100, or 75 / 70 / 60 for a few). |
| 2026-11-15 | 1.9 | **music: which track, traced.** In a game this console runs (single player or the host - the session's script callback is set, func 08970FB0; the offline game starts set it, 08A2ECB8 and the others) the level script picks the match music: a timer entity (ENTI type 1, version 4, 0 s at +0x30) fires its message group [2] (SMSG: u32 groups; u32 side, version >= 2 - the entities use side 0; a u8 count per group; messages of u16 type, u16 extra size, u32 target, 3 more words in version 6, then the extra bytes padded to 4 when the chunk's flag bit 0 is clear) whose message 5 (activate) reaches a selector (type 0xB: u32 version, flags, n, n x { group | 0x10000 enabled, weight }; flags 1 = one weighted random pick, func 08870E0C; 8 = in turn; else all), whose entries are groups activating music entities (type 0x1F: version, list hash, flags; func 0886F41C sends 0x72 "play the list" for flag 0x10 and 0x73 "go to state" 4 / 7 / 10 for flag 2 / 4 / 8). Every Classic and Prequel level carries the same six: "OGM Music 1" or "OGM Music 2" (hash 0x4A32E16B / 0x4A32E16C) at state 4, 7 or 10, weight 1 each. The music player (088D61F4 / 088D66EC / 088D6A3C): a list's MMUS record (name; hash; word 10 the start state, 4; 13 states x { exists, 0, 0, file 1-based }) has its loops at states 4, 7 and 10 - files 1, 2 and 3 - and the transitions 5 / 6 / 8 / 9 / 11 / 12 between them (absent in these levels); states without a segment are skipped, so the picked loop starts at once and re-queues itself for the whole match. No other message reaches the music entities, so there are no mid-match changes; the end plays VICTORY or FAIL (08A09620). A network client (no script) picks "OGM Music 1" or "2" at state 4 itself (08972170); ours falls back to that when a level has no script (the campaign levels script their music separately: not yet). `AK_MUSIC_LIST` / `AK_MUSIC_TRACK` now only force a choice. |
| 2026-11-15 | 1.9 | **speech: narration and AI chatter, traced.** Data: GMSND.ASR's DLEV chunks are the dialogue events (name, padded to 4; u32 kind; 0; 0x16464; 0; 0; then byte-packed: u32 count at +23 and n x { u32 line hash; 0; 0; 1; 0; u8 0; line name; 3 zero bytes }; the line hash is h31 of the line's path with '/' for '\\'); GMSND.AM's DLLN chunks are the lines (name; sound hash at +8; seconds at +16; the UTF-16 subtitle text, empty for chatter), and its "streamed sounds" RSCF (file offset 122082) maps sound hashes to the ATRAC voice data. As the media rule says, the voice plays only from the converted WAVs (converted/PSP_GAME/USRDIR/SOUNDS/GMSND.AM.d/<file offset as %08X>.wav, mono 44.1 kHz). Which event a side says comes from its Humanoid blueprint's Speech_BN_* (narrator) and Speech_BC_* (chatter) keys, e.g. IMP_BN_CP_Lost. **When, from the code:** a command post changing hands (func 089EA3D0) - ours lost: CP_Lost; taken by us: CP_Gained; taken by the enemy from neutral: CP_Enemy; the tickets (089882BC) - a lead over 30: Reinforcements once to the side behind, or, with the side behind under 20, Loosing / Winning once; one narrator line at a time, flat. Chatter (message 0x807F to the speaker, AI only): Hit (089E22BC) - a hit over 0.4 of full health always, else only below half health on a coin toss, then 3 s quiet (constants 0x08AEF210..21C); Killed (089E28B4) and Attack, on first sighting an enemy (0893254C), each on a coin toss; positional at the head, the game's "AI Speech" list (gain 0.7, full to 1 m, gone at 50 m). Checked on Classic Hoth with 63 bots: CP_Lost / CP_Gained / CP_Enemy lines with their text, IMP_ / ALL_BC_Attack, BC_Hit, Wookie_Hiss. Subtitles: the line text is loaded (`AK_SUBTITLES=1` shows it at the bottom), off by default until the game's own subtitle display is traced. `AK_DEBUG_SPEECH=1` logs each line. |
| 2026-11-15 | 1.9 | **audio: the game's sounds and music.** SOUNDS/GMSND.ASR is the shared bank, each level adds its own: SDSM chunks are samples (name; id; the RSCF type-3 .wav resource, PSP VAG ADPCM; gains; mixer group), SDEV chunks are events (name; volume min / max; pitch min / max; 0; 0; 0.5; 1; 1; a count 2-4; 0x100; 0; distance min / max; n sample hashes - h31 of SDSM names, one picked per play), SDMX mixer groups, DLEV / DLLN dialogue lines (GMSND.AM: Battle Narration, AI Speech). audio/ak_audio.c: 48 voices, 48 kHz stereo, linear resampling, the event's random volume and pitch, a fade between its two distances, pan to the camera; level events replace shared ones of the same name. Output: SDL on desktop, the main sceAudioOut port on the Vita (256-frame grains on its own thread). **Surfaces, from the data:** a collision triangle's material number indexes the level's render materials, whose mode word is the surface - 0 Default, 1 Snow, 2 Sand, 3 Grass, 4 Wood, 5 Stone, 6 Metal, 7 Water, 8 Soft, 9 Marsh (the Material blueprints' order, Default first; checked on Hoth, Tatooine, Kashyyyk, Endor) - and each Material blueprint names footstep_soldier, jumpland_soldier, roll_soldier, bodyfall_soldier and "laser impact sound". Played: weapons' "Fire Sound" (hand weapons, vehicle and turret hardpoint Weapons), bolt hits by surface, a footstep a stride (1.9 m, from the run clip's root motion), landings, deaths (the Humanoid blueprint's "Death Sound": stormtrooper_death, Rebel_Death, Clone_death, Droid_Death, Wookie_Death) and body falls, vehicle "Engine Sound" loops while driven or walking their path, Command_post_hum and Command_Post_Change, the level's ambience (Amb_<level>_Ext_C / L / R, flat). **Music:** the level's MMUS chunk lists VICTORY, FAIL, "OGM Music 1" and "OGM Music 2" (three tracks each). The tracks are ATRAC3plus (.AT3 in SOUNDS/MUSIC: RIFF, 44.1 kHz stereo, 744-byte packets of 2048 samples; fact = total and encoder delay, smpl = the loop); as the project's rule, they play only from the converted WAV copies the media conversion writes (platform/common/convert.c: converted/PSP_GAME/USRDIR/SOUNDS/MUSIC/<NAME>.AT3.wav in a data folder - on the Vita ux0:data/SWBFRS/converted, written by the old port's first-boot screen; every ATRAC frame in order, so the original's sample positions hold). The engine reads only the original's header for the delay and the loop points, never decodes it, and streams the WAV on the audio thread in 32 KB pieces. The match plays the level script's pick (the music row above), and the level's VICTORY or FAIL when it ends. (Speech, which music track and the mixer: the rows above.) |

A 1 s pull-up ends at 38.5° (Aurek 37.5°) and a 0.5 s push brings it to 20.0° (Aurek 20.0°); a 2 s full turn covers 135.3° (Aurek 133.6°). **Engine:** flyers lift off along their takeoff clip, start at the stall speed, use R / L as the two speed settings, and turn with rate dynamics on pitch (and on yaw for craft that do not roll). The player's flyer takes off on R only; bots still lift off on any control and keep the boost. Viewer: the script's `l` also presses R on its first frame. The per-frame speed is exactly the clips' key slopes: idle_to_leftfoot (0.5 s at 3.53 m/s), then the walk loop (7.1, 8.8, 5.1, 3.9 m/s ...). The speed is the magnitude of the root velocity across the ground (the clips' sideways sway counts in the speed but does not move it sideways). On release it walks on to the loop's next foot point (end = left foot, middle = right foot), then steps down with leftfoot_ / rightfoot_to_idle; both release points in the run matched. The turn starts at 4.8 degrees/s, spins up at 1 rad/s² to MaxTurnYaw (28.65 degrees/s) and stops at 4 rad/s². **Engine:** walkers driven by the player or bots now walk this way when their model has the clips (others keep the earlier steady walk) and are posed from the clip being walked. | 2026-11-22 | platforms | **PS4: toolchain built from source; renderer decision pending.** The OpenOrbis release archives cannot be downloaded here, so `platform/ps4/bootstrap_toolchain.sh` builds an equivalent from git: system-library stubs (sources from toolchain commit c946047^), the OpenOrbis musl, crt1, libc++ 11 / libc++abi / libunwind from the OpenOrbis LLVM fork (merged into one libc++.a), create-fself / create-gp4 (Go) and PkgTool.Core (.NET 8). Checked: a C test links and becomes an eboot.bin. Renderer findings: Piglet (the system GLES 2) starts only in system-category (gde) packages (~768 MiB) and has no shader compiler on retail consoles - the runtime compiler libSceShaccVSH.sprx is a devkit module, so shaders must ship as binaries; no reachable offline compiler produces Piglet's format (psbc is on gitgud.io, which refuses this host). The alternative is PS4GL_GNM (github markabrazuk336-maker/PS4GL_GNM): GLES 2-style calls over GNM, the console's native GPU API, shaders compiled on the PC with LLVM's AMDGPU back end, a normal game package (~4.5 GiB), confirmed on a PS4 Pro by its author [not tested on a base PS4 by them]. Running its build tools here was refused by the session's safety check (third-party code), so it waits for the owner's go-ahead. |

| Movie subtitles (M2) | `SUBTITLE/<NAME>.ASR` (one SUBT chunk: u32 count, then {f32 start, f32 end, u32 text key}) with the texts from `MISC/CUTSCENE/CUTSCENE_AM.ASR` (HTXT table 0x25C2AF8A), loaded only for FE_PlayMovie with BFF.Subtitles on (08A69188). Drawn over the picture through the new `ak_movie_set_overlay` hook: the line with start <= t < end, alpha = min(t - start, end - t) x 4 capped at 1 (0884999C, constants 0x08AEBA18/1C), white, Franklin Gothic Medium Cond 9 at 0.8 (08A69920 / 0x08AEBA28), centred in x 0.075..0.925, y 0.89..0.98 with flags 0x44 (08A6994C). A '{' or '}' first character always shows, minus the mark; other lines need +0x3F (0897A350). The movie time is the sound clock (or the wall clock without sound). Test hook: `AK_MOVIE_TEST_SUBS=1`. |

| Difficulty page (M2) | MP_AIDifficulty's OnOpen FE_AIDifficultyOnInit (08A2EB9C) puts the focus on W_BU_NormalDifficulty (0xABD00B76) when the profile's +0x34 is below 2, otherwise on W_BU_EliteDifficulty (0x1370447A). The buttons' action BFF.SelectDifficulty (0897968C) stores 1 (normal) or 2 (elite) at +0x34; any other value is ignored, and +6 (profile changed) is set when the value differs. The single player's difficulty is +0x34 (08979DF4; multiplayer reads 0x08C294DC instead), so AK_DIFFICULTY now follows the profile, both when it is loaded and when this button is pressed. The handler names were found as UTF-16 strings in BOOT.BIN (0x08AE09C0, 0x08ADD554) registered by 08897664 / 088976C4. |

| Delete profile and Cutscenes list (M2) | (1) FE_DeletePlayerProfile (08A2EB0C -> 08A62294 -> 08A6A234 -> 0890E49C) runs the save utility in mode 7: it deletes the profile's own save after the system asks first. It is not the list dialogue, and the profile in memory stays. Aurek now asks "Delete this profile?" on the current slot: Yes deletes it (result 2), No or Back cancels (result 4), and an empty slot fails (result 3). Before, it deleted at once and dropped the profile from memory. (2) FE_ListAvaliableCutscenes (08A2EF58) fills MP_MCutscenes' list (20 rows, 8 shown) from the movie table (0x08B1C08C). Row k is shown while k <= max(+0x5DC campaign level, +0x5E0 cutscenes unlocked), and its text is the table's. A new profile lists only Intro. The movie table moved ahead of the list fillers. |

| Autosave on leaving Options (M2) | MP_Options_FrontEnd's Back targets MP_OptionsAutosave. That page's OnOpen is FE_SavePlayerProfile "1" and its OnClose is FE_ExitAutoSaveMenu. Its "autoclick if not needed" and "autoclick if autosave success" buttons (flags 7, +153, time 0) press themselves as soon as their conditions show them. They go back to the main menu with the focus on W_BU_MMOptions, as the original does. The fix: self-pressing buttons (0888D170: flags bit 1, +153, the page's active time >= +196) are only considered while their conditions hold, since the original only updates shown widgets. A time of 0 now counts (it was skipped before). |

| Single Player entry pages; M2 complete | (1) FE_CheckConquestState (08A2EC80) does nothing: the New button's target opens MP_Overwrite on "No". It no longer starts a conquest itself. (2) FE_CheckCampaignState (08A2EC10) starts the campaign only when the profile has no progress (0x08C29918 = profile +0x5DC is 0); otherwise the button's target, MP_Overwrite, asks first. (3) The name-based "W_BU_AutoClick*" rule ([assumed]) is removed. MP_GalacticConquest's W_BU_AutoClickWhenNoSavedConquest now presses itself by the traced rule (flags 3, +153, time 0, while BFF.HasGalacticConquestBeenPlayed == 0), and a log line names every self-pressing button. (4) Checked side by side with the harness original: splash -> new profile -> difficulty -> save page -> Continue Without Saving -> main menu -> Single Player -> Instant Action map list. Every page, its focus and the map list match. |

| Customization menu logos (M3) | Each Personalize button on MP_MCustomisationMenu holds four faction logos from gc_tech_elements.tga. One has flags 0x43F and the other three have flags 0. Aurek drew all four on top of each other, which read as a white circle. Pictures with no flags at all are now hidden, so each button shows its own faction's emblem as in the original's frame. Compared side by side: the Customization menu and the load-out page now match. Still missing: the carousel's 3D item models and the soldier preview. |

| Menu models (M3) | The original opens no level for the menus (strace on the harness original as far as the load-out page: no ENVS file read). The customisation menu's models are in BFF_MAINMENU.GUI itself: 234 meshes (soldiers by faction and class with their L1 / L2 levels of detail, skeletons, the items). Aurek now loads them at front-end start into their own arena (AK_FE_MODELS, AK_FE_MODELS_MB 12). The file has no level geometry, so ak_level_parse's false is ignored once its textures are read. Only its textures go to the GPU, as a scene level of textures alone (viewer.c fe_scene_textures). The model widgets (carousel items, second weapon) draw from these models when no level is loaded. Before, they were empty until a level had been played. |

| Menu item model names (M3) | Some items' WeaponModel is a path ("Objects\\Weapons\\EMP_Blaster_rifle\\EMP_Blaster_rifle.ASR"), while the menu file names its mesh bare ("EMP_Blaster_rifle"). The menu's model lookup now also tries the path's last part without .ASR. Under AK_LOG=2, every menu item's model is logged once as found or MISSING. Before this, the Empire rifle, thermal detonator, detpack and auto-turret pack had no model in the menu. |

| Loadout in the profile (M3) | The menu's loadout now lives in the profile, as in the original. 08977A34(profile, set, category, value) stores set k (< 20) at +0x1B4 + 44 k, category c (< 8) at +4 + 4 c, and marks the profile changed (+6) only when the value differs. The menu uses set 0. FE_Customisation_AcceptCarouselItem (08A27F28) and SellCarouselItem (08A27FD8, value 0) store through it. OnInit (08A276F0) reads the menu's choices from the profile. Cancel stores the loadout as it was. ak_fe_loadout, which the spawn reads, takes set 0 from the profile. Values out of a category's range fall back to 0. The new profile's set 0 is 08977ED8's {1, 0, 1, 0, 0, 1, 1, 0}, the menu's old defaults. Checked in Aurek: Weapon 1 changed to its third item logs "category 0 = 2 (profile +0x1B8)", and the change survives leaving and reopening the menu. |

| Loadout at match start (M3) | Aurek applied the customisation menu's loadout only when that menu closed during a match (AK_FE_LOADOUT). A match started from the menus, or the next map of a play list, spawned the soldier with the settings' defaults instead of the saved profile loadout. viewer.c's new fe_loadout_settings() turns set 0 of the profile (via ak_fe_loadout) into the soldier's equipment settings: weapons, explosive, special item, power-up, and the health, speed and capture levels. It runs on FE_HostGame's start, on the play list's next map, and before ak_play_loadout when the menu closes in a match. |

| M3 complete (customisation and loadouts) | Customization menu (faction logos), load-out lists, costs and credits match the original side by side; the menus' own models load from BFF_MAINMENU.GUI (no level needed); item models are found by path or name; the loadout lives in the profile as the original's (set 0 at +0x1B8, 08977A34; credits = total - chosen costs, 08A34290) and reaches the soldier at every match start. Open (PARITY_TASKS): the original's own set-0-to-spawn path, a byte check against the original's save, the soldier preview without a level, the carousel placement. |

| Loadout selection rules (M4) | Traced: (1) taking an item (08A27F28) has no credit check - no affordability variable exists among the menu's BFF.FE_Customisation_* names; the credits shown (08A27994) are total - remaining with remaining = 08A34290's total minus the 8 chosen costs, so spending can pass the total; (2) selling (08A27FD8) is allowed only while the flag behind BFF.FE_Customisation_EnableSellButton (0x08C2CEA8) is set, stores 0 for the category, recomputes the credits and returns to MP_Customisation (0x820C81DE); (3) both mark the profile changed (+6 = 1) whatever the value - Aurek now does too (it only did when the value differed). |

| Loadout allowance and faction versions (M4) | (1) The allowance is 100: 08A34280 sets the credits' allowance and what is left to 100. The 200 seen in the harness original came from the recomp's own RS_CREDITS hook (platform/common/loadout_slots.c, rs_credits_init after 08A34280), together with its RS_ANY_SLOT list extension. Aurek's allowance is now AK_CREDITS, default 100, as the original. fe_compare.py now runs the original with RS_CREDITS=100 RS_ANY_SLOT=0 so comparisons are against the unmodded game. (2) Over the allowance: taking items has no check, but leaving the menu with less than 0 left opens MP_CustomOverSpent (its W_BU_Back_OverSpent shows while BFF.FE_Customisation_Credits < 0); Aurek already did this. (3) Faction versions (08A23598): faction 4 returns the item itself; otherwise, when the item's "Faction Specific" (0x10674EB6) is set, its component 0xD0CDC21C's children are searched for "Faction" (0xBEEB1A1C, default 4) equal to the faction. Aurek's cust_item does the same, with the child default now 4. The menu's faction (08A28C68) is the player's (+24) during a match, else the last kept, which starts at 2 (0x08B1BE00). |

| Spawn kit path (M4 complete) | The original's path from profile set 0 to the soldier is now traced. A method of the match's player object, 08A08EF8 (vtable 0x08B1473C, the object built by 08A089B8 from 08971770), fills a kit record. It holds four values from the profile's levels: +168 = 08978170, from category 5 (+24) through 0x08AEE3B8 {100, 133, 166, 200}; +172 = 089781D8, from category 7 (+32) through 0x08AEE3C8 {100, 133, 166, 200}; +176 = 08978240, from category 6 (+28) through 0x08AEE3D8 {0.85, 1, 1.15, 1.3}; +180 = 089782A8, from category 7 (+32) through 0x08AEE3E8 {0.5, 1, 1.5, 2}. Then 08978310 adds the 5 item ids: categories 0..4 through the tables 0x08ADC5E8 / 610 / 630 / 648 / 668, only while the profile's +0x1B0 flag is set. Level 0 takes each table's first value. Aurek's level tables (play.c loadout_levels) are the same values. |

| Instant Action flow, first check (M5) | Compared side by side from the Instant Action map list: the Planet, Mode (Conquest) and Era (Galactic Civil War) pages, the play list line ("Boz Pity - CQST - GCW"), Launch and the Team Selection page all match in layout and texts. Fixed: the team columns' faction logos (E_IM_StyleButtonH_Logo_* in GUIMENU_INGAMESP, flags 0, picked by faction in a match by 08A2A844) were hidden by the flag-0 picture rule. That rule now leaves "_Logo_" pictures alone during a match. Found: the original's Team Selection lists 8 + 8 bots, Aurek's 8 + 7 (AK_BOTS defaults to 15). The selected list row is dimmer in the original's mode, era and planet lists. |
